Layer 3 and Layer 4

Layer 3: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months
and Layer 4: Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

10 questions in 5 groups · 12,407 words · written 24 October 2026, 01:40 to 02:05 UK, Fable, ricky login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes for this layer

The Investment Analyst's title
Asker Healthcare (ASKER-SE): Third and fourth sets of questions

Written 2026-10-24 for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo. Money is in SEK unless stated. "Adjusted EBITA" is the company's own measure. "Model basis" EPS is adjusted EBITA less net financial items, taxed at 24%, over the diluted share count, as the Financial Analyst's model computes it. "Brokers' basis", "FactSet" and consensus figures are from the financials block of 2026-10-22 (three brokers). Last reported period: Q2 2026; Q3 2026 results are due 4 November 2026. Share price SEK 70.8 on 21 October 2026; the model uses SEK 71.70 on 22 October.

How to read this set

Three preliminary points condition every answer below.

First, the cross-check gap in the model summary. The summary flags that the second set's own free cash flow (SEK 483m, 1,070m, 1,352m, 1,537m and 1,672m for 2026 to 2030) exceeds the model's (SEK 377m, 950m, 1,228m, 1,406m and 1,538m) by SEK 106m to 134m in every year, while EPS matches to the öre. I have reconciled the gap exactly: it is the tax line and nothing else. The model charges tax at 24% on adjusted pre-tax profit (adjusted EBITA less net financial items), which is SEK 376m in 2026, 430m in 2027, 504m in 2028, 573m in 2029 and 638m in 2030; the second set's cross-check charged 24% on reported pre-tax profit, after about SEK 400m to 490m of acquired-intangible amortisation and SEK 60m to 70m of items affecting comparability, which is SEK 270m, 310m, 380m, 443m and 504m. The differences (SEK 106m, 120m, 124m, 130m and 134m) equal the gaps in each year. No input is wrong on this account: the two figures are two conventions for cash tax, and the truth sits between them. Asker buys companies as share purchases, so the amortisation of acquired customer relationships and brands is in the main not deductible for cash tax, and the income-statement tax rate of 22.6% in 2025 looks normal only because the deferred tax liability set up on those intangibles unwinds through the tax line; that argues that the model's higher charge is the closer proxy for cash tax. Against that, FactSet's "Viewforth definition" net income for 2025 (SEK 913m against a pre-amortisation pre-tax profit of about SEK 1,030m) implies a current tax charge of only about SEK 120m, which argues the other way, although 2025 carried IPO costs and the last of the shareholder-loan interest. I therefore keep the 24% tax input, treat the model's free cash flow as the figure of record and the second set's as the upper bound, and ask the Consumer to carry the range (SEK 0.4bn to 0.5bn in 2026; 0.95bn to 1.07bn in 2027; 1.23bn to 1.35bn in 2028). Research request OWN-1 asks for the cash-tax evidence. The consequence that does matter is for leverage: on the model's lower cash flow, net debt does not stay flat as the second set assumed but drifts up by about SEK 420m in 2027, 300m in 2028, 230m in 2029 and 210m in 2030 after acquisitions and dividends, so the company-definition leverage path is about 2.25x, 2.2x, 2.0x, 1.9x and 1.8x at the ends of 2026 to 2030 rather than 2.25x, 2.1x, 1.85x, 1.65x and 1.5x. It stays inside the 2.5x ceiling and keeps falling, but leverage falls below 2.0x in 2029 rather than 2028. That makes the second set's net financial items input (falling from SEK 340m to 300m by 2030 on flat net debt) inconsistent with the model's own cash flow, and I change that one input (see inputs-changed.md).

Second, two things in the model summary the Consumer should verify with the Financial Analyst rather than quote. (a) The model's year-end 2026 net debt of SEK 6,780m (its lease-inclusive, FactSet-style definition) is about SEK 1bn above what the June 2026 balance of SEK 5,699m plus the second half's flows produces: second-half free cash flow of roughly SEK 250m to 300m against about SEK 370m of second-half acquisitions and a small translation increase on euro debt gives about SEK 5,800m to 5,850m lease-inclusive, or about SEK 4,700m on the company's definition, which is the second set's figure. The pattern is consistent with the model applying full-year 2026 cash flows to the mid-year balance. It does not affect EPS, the P/E-based return or any input, but it should not be quoted as the leverage of record. (b) The return table shows a "guideline fair multiple" of 0.00x while computing a value of SEK 111.33 at the 18-month column; that value is consistent with about 24x applied to 24-month-forward EPS at December 2027 (the model's 2029 EPS of SEK 4.70), that is, the median of the stock's own forward P/E history (24.0x). The 0.00x is a display fault. The substantive point, which belongs to the memo's topics #11 and #12 and which I take up in D-07 and the fourth set's judgement, is that this "history" is nineteen months of listed life, most of it at the premium rating the stock carried before September 2026, so the median is the pre-dispute multiple, and 45 of the 55 points of 18-month return in the model come from regaining it.

Third, where I differ from the earlier sets. I agree with the second set's five corrections of the first and build on them. I differ in four places, each argued in the answers. (i) The first set's base case described a voluntary accounting clean-up costing 3 to 6 points of 2027 adjusted EBITA growth; the second set modelled about one point. I keep the second set's inputs but the Consumer should know the model sits at the optimistic end of the first set's own description (D-01). (ii) The second set wrote that the rolling 12-month adjusted EBITA margin crosses 10% at Q2 or Q3 2027; on its own quarterly margin inputs it does not: the rolling margin is 9.76% after Q2 2027, 9.85% after Q3 2027 and touches 10.0% only at the FY2027 report in February 2028. I adopt the arithmetic, which moves the "first target met" event to the edge of the 18-month window (D-01, I-01). (iii) The AlphaSense reports written for this set, which draw four-fifths of their material from DNB Carnegie's notes of 10 September and 1 October 2026, describe the deleveraging path as "extremely narrow" and the covenant as nearly binding; the covenant report commissioned by the second set shows the bank's definition excludes leases and long-term earn-outs and takes acquired EBITDA pro forma, so the company's alternative ratio of 2.43x sits more than a turn below the 3.50x limit. I treat a forced stop as remote and a chosen slowdown as the risk (D-02, I-03). (iv) About two-thirds to 70% of the 2027 adjusted EBITA growth the model forecasts is already secured by the organic engine and the annualisation of the 2026 acquisitions, so the market's dominant fear, that the deal engine stops, is a 2028 to 2030 risk and not a 2027 one (D-02, D-03, I-03).

Layer 3: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months

Risks (the third set)

7 questions
D-0166D-0258D-0355D-0448D-0554D-0647D-0756
D-01

How congruent are the answers to the earlier sets?

C66

Question asked How congruent are the answers to all the questions in the earlier sets: to what extent do they support and align with each other, rather than contradict or jar?

Judgement: highly congruent. The first and second sets rest on one spine, stated in the same words in both: statutory cash conversion governs leverage headroom, headroom governs the acquisition budget, and the budget governs more than half of adjusted EBITA growth, so the model has one dominant input and it is the one the company is currently failing on. Around that spine the operating answers (market growth of 3 to 4% plus 1 to 2 points of channel shift; organic sales growth of 4.5 to 5%; a gross margin that is a mix path with no real pricing; distribution-grade, bounded moats; a strong operator with a loose reporting posture) and the financial answers (adjusted margin to 10% in 2027 and 10.4% in 2028; a cash margin 1.3 to 1.5 points lower; EPS leaking through interest and amortisation; a 2026 free-cash-flow trough) support each other without strain. The second set corrected the first in five places and every correction moved in the same direction, toward more caution on cash and leverage and unchanged confidence in operations, which is itself evidence of coherence rather than drift. I find five jars, all resolvable, and one place where both sets are congruent with each other but not with the model, the tax convention explained above.

The jars, in order of consequence for the memo.

(1) The size of the accounting clean-up in the base case. The first set's Management and Risks judgements call a voluntary clean-up in the FY2026 report "the more likely path" and tell the model to carry "a 3 to 6 point one-off drag on 2027 adjusted EBITA growth", with 25 to 35% probability on a mild form and 10 to 15% on a severe one. The second set carried SEK 15m of year-end prudence in Q4 2026 and SEK 25m of higher provisioning run-rate in 2027, about 1.2 points of 2027 growth, and explicitly declined DNB Carnegie's SEK 120m catch-up. These are not the same base case. The reconciliation is probabilistic: a disclosed one-off of SEK 100m to 150m at a 25 to 35% probability has an expected value of SEK 30m to 50m, close to what the second set carries, so the model is right as a probability-weighted path and optimistic as a modal description of what the first set expected. I keep the second set's inputs, because the model has no bear case and the mild clean-up belongs in a named scenario, but the memo should state that the 2027 adjusted EBITA of SEK 2,134m is SEK 40m to 100m above the figure the first set's own base-case language implies. This is the largest internal difference between the sets.

(2) When the margin crosses 10%. The first set put the rolling 12-month adjusted EBITA margin above 10% at Q4 2026 or Q1 2027; the second set, after adding Gothenburg double-running costs and the provisioning drag, wrote Q2 or Q3 2027; its own quarterly inputs (9.6%, 9.8%, 9.7%, 9.9%, 10.0%, 10.3% for Q3 2026 to Q4 2027 on revenue of about SEK 4,750m, 5,420m, 5,060m, 5,160m, 5,220m and 5,910m) give a rolling margin of 9.76% after Q2 2027, 9.85% after Q3 2027 and 9.99% after Q4 2027. The target is reached, in the sense the company would claim it, at the FY2027 report in February 2028. This matters for the fourth set: the "first target met" narrative event now coincides not with the FY2026 accounting disclosures, which it would have softened, but with the end of the window, and the mid-2027 reports instead carry the first "rolling growth below 15%" headline (I-01).

(3) Net financial items. The first set (C-07) said to model them "flat to slightly up" with no rate relief; the second set modelled them flat to 2027 and then falling to SEK 300m by 2030 on the argument that absolute net debt stays flat. On the model's own cash flow net debt does not stay flat; the Riksbank is expected to hike in November 2026; the krona's weakness since September raises the kronor cost of euro debt; lease interest rises with the estate; and the margin-grid step-downs disclosed in the prospectus are earned only as leverage falls, which on the model's cash flow reaches 2.0x in 2029. The first set's instruction was the right one and I restore it.

(4) The acquisition contribution. The first set (C-18, C-85) would model acquired EBITA growth at 7 to 9% for 2027 to 2028 and read management's commentary as supporting a figure "above the broker's 7% and below the framework's 10 to 12%"; the second set modelled 7% in 2027, 6 to 7% thereafter and an acquired sales contribution of 4.5% by 2030, at or below the bottom of that range. The second set's figure follows from its cash budget and I accept it as the base case, but the model is at the cautious end of the first set's range and below every management signal; if cash conversion recovers to the 2022 to 2024 level, acquired growth of 9 to 10% is affordable and 2028 to 2030 EBITA is 5 to 8% higher than the model.

(5) The deleveraging path. The second set's 2.25x, 2.1x, 1.85x rests on its own (higher) cash flow; the model's cash flow gives 2.25x, 2.2x, 2.0x. Both are inside the ceiling; the difference is a year in the timing of the engine's re-engagement and is already covered in the preliminary note.

What is congruent and important. Both sets agree on the three dated, observable items that decide the case (statutory operating cash flow and inventory days at the FY2026 report; the FY2026 accounts' treatment of provisions and capitalisation; the Q3 2027 arrival of Gothenburg savings); on the four EPS bases and the instruction never to compare the model's EPS with the FactSet median except as growth rates; on the fair trough (SEK 45 to 50) and the upside case (SEK 95 to 105); and on AddLife's 2023 to 2025 path as the template. The AlphaSense reports written for this set are congruent with the sets in their facts and more negative in their net reads; the gap is explained by their source mix (DNB Carnegie's forensic note and preview supply most of the cited material, and the reports repeat the broker's 3.1 to 3.4x all-in leverage as if it were the covenant measure, which the covenant report shows it is not). Where the reports and the sets differ, I weight the sets.

Why congruence matters for predictability, and its limit. A model whose parts agree can be wrong together, and the sets share one assumption that, if wrong, moves every line at once: that management will choose to hold the 2.5x ceiling and set the deal budget from cash. If management instead spends to the ceiling, which the covenant permits, 2027 to 2028 EBITA is higher and leverage, interest and the multiple are worse; if the new Chair forces faster deleveraging, EBITA is lower and the multiple better. The sets' congruence is conditional on a management choice that the July 2026 language ("gradual realisation of benefits", "focus on cash flow and capital efficiency") supports but does not prove, and the memo should say so.

D-02

Does past and present delivery make the expected change plausible, and does the thesis rely on demonstrated strengths?

C58

Question asked To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

Judgement: the expected change is plausible on the record, and the thesis rests mostly on strengths the business has demonstrated, with one important exception that governs 2028 to 2030 rather than 2027. The change expected is (i) organic sales growth of 4.5 to 5% with organic adjusted EBITA growth of 6 to 7%; (ii) an acquired EBITA contribution of about 7% in 2027 and 6 to 7% thereafter, funded from cash flow while leverage falls; (iii) a margin rising about 30 basis points a year from mix, pruning and Gothenburg, through a dip from Q3 2026 to Q2 2027; (iv) free cash flow before acquisitions rising from about SEK 0.4bn in 2026 to SEK 1.2bn to 1.35bn in 2028 as capex, earn-outs and inventory normalise; (v) leverage falling with net debt roughly flat.

Demonstrated strengths that carry the forecast. The organic engine is demonstrated over six quarters (6, 3, 5, -1, 5 and 5%, the two outliers explained by the 2024 defence orders), by an 88% re-win rate and a 69% total tender win rate in the Nordics (prospectus), by the Dutch platform's growth from 80,000 to 570,000 patients, and by a market growing 3 to 4%; the organic EBITA gearing of 1.3 to 1.4x is demonstrated in H1 2026 (7% on 5%) and matches management's own framework. Acquisition sourcing, pricing and integration are demonstrated beyond doubt: more than 60 deals at 6 to 8x EBITA, a three-year incremental return of about 23% (Carnegie), H1 2026 acquisitions consolidated at a 17% EBITA margin, a deal terminated when the process failed, 14 deals signed in 2025 and seven to eight more in 2026. Mix-driven margin improvement is demonstrated: Central's margin from 7.8% to 9.2% through pruning, the 110 basis point gross-margin gain in Q2 2026 that the CFO attributed to acquired mix and cost of goods, North at 13.6%. Working-capital steering is demonstrated as a culture (return on net working capital 69% against a 50% target; AddLife 62%) but not as inventory discipline: inventory days went from 59 to 65 in 2022 to 75 in 2025 and 82 in Q2 2026, gross inventories compounded at 14% against cost of goods at 10% over 2021 to 2025 (DNB Carnegie), and Carnegie's observation that the metric has been held by extending supplier terms rather than by turns is the right reading. Logistics consolidation is half-demonstrated: the Dutch relocation was completed in late 2025 with short-term costs, and Gothenburg was reported "fully on schedule" in July 2026 after testing began in April, but the company has never run an automated centre of this size.

Strengths the forecast would need the company to acquire. (a) Deleveraging while acquiring. No member of the cohort has lowered leverage from about 2.4x to below 2.0x while adding 6 to 7% of EBITA a year from acquisitions; AddLife (3.5x at end-2023) and Storskogen (4.35x) both stopped for about two years, and Vimian needed a SEK 1.5bn directed issue. Asker's own record is that it ran at about 2.3x through 2022 to 2024 while acquiring at a far higher pace, with operating cash conversion of about 101% of adjusted EBITA, so the capability existed at smaller scale and under private ownership; what is undemonstrated is the same capability at twice the size, with SEK 645m of contingent consideration outstanding and inventory days at 82. (b) Restoring inventory efficiency after an acquisition wave. AddLife took until late 2023 to release cash after its 2021 wave; Vimian's programme took about two years; Lifco's subsidiaries resisted reducing buffers for several quarters. Asker's inventory days have risen for four years; the second set's path (80 at end-2026, 76 at end-2027, 72 at end-2028) requires a reversal the company has not yet shown, and it is worth about SEK 150m to 200m of cash a year. (c) Conservative reporting without an earnings shock. Not demonstrated; the record to date runs the other way (provisions down, useful lives extended, integration costs adjusted out). The new Chair and the principal owner's representative on the Audit Committee are the agents, not yet the evidence. (d) Founder retention after the 2022 to 2023 cohort's earn-outs expire. Undisclosed; the research commissioned on it (OWN-3 of the second set) found no subsidiary-level evidence either way; the SEK 463m paid in Q2 2026 says the cohort outperformed its targets, which is the best available sign that founders stayed and performed through the earn-out period.

How much of the forecast rests on each, in numbers. The useful arithmetic is for 2027, the year the fourth set turns on. The model's 2027 adjusted EBITA growth of 12.5% is SEK 238m. Organic EBITA growth of 6 to 7% supplies SEK 115m to 130m (demonstrated). The seven 2026 acquisitions carry about SEK 1.3bn of annual sales, of which about SEK 860m falls in 2026 and about SEK 435m annualises into 2027 (Van Heek and GHC/MPF from February, RMS from April, Murray Surgical from July, Med Kjemi from September, Ambercell and Labo Clinic late in the year); at a 10% margin that is about SEK 45m of EBITA, 2.3 points of growth, secured without a single new deal. Gothenburg and provisioning net to about nil in 2027. Together the secured items are SEK 160m to 175m, two-thirds to 70% of the forecast. The remaining SEK 65m to 80m of in-year 2027 EBITA requires new acquisitions of roughly SEK 0.9bn to 1.0bn signed by mid-2027 (because a deal closed mid-year contributes half a year), which is exactly the second set's budget of SEK 950m. So the floors are these: with the deal engine fully stopped from today, 2027 adjusted EBITA growth is about 8 to 9%; with deals funded only from the SEK 569m of undrawn facilities and the roughly SEK 500m of headroom under the 2.5x ceiling, about 11 to 12%; the base case of 12.5% needs the SEK 950m budget, which needs free cash flow of roughly SEK 0.9bn after dividends or a willingness to let leverage sit at 2.3x for a year. For 2028 to 2030 the proportions shift: the acquired contribution of 6 to 7% a year is then entirely new money, funded by a cash-conversion recovery that is not yet demonstrated, and the share of those years' growth that rests on demonstrated strengths is 55 to 60%.

The thesis's merit and its weakness. Its merit is that it asks for nothing the company lacks in kind: no new product category, no greenfield market, no pricing power, no central cost programme, no change of model; every lever is one the company has pulled before. Its weakness is that it asks the two capabilities that have deteriorated most in the last two years (inventory discipline and cash conversion) to recover while the company runs its first large logistics consolidation and answers a public accounting challenge, and it asks the one capability the company has never shown (conservative reporting) to appear without a shock. The AlphaSense report's "extremely narrow path requiring flawless execution" is too strong for 2027: those numbers do not require flawless execution, and the covenant (3.50x on a definition that excludes leases and long-term earn-outs, against an alternative ratio of 2.43x) means a stumble produces a chosen slowdown, not a forced one. The 2028 to 2030 numbers do require the cash recovery, and that is where plausibility is only moderate.

D-03

How diffuse or concentrated are the drivers of the difference between forecast and history?

C55

Question asked In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years, vs. the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?

Judgement: concentrated for growth, diffuse for margin, and concentrated in three dated items for cash. The concentration is in a variable that is observable every quarter, partly already secured for 2027, and controlled by a management decision rather than by the market; that is better for predictability than a diffuse set of external drivers would be, at the price of a two-state character (engine running, engine throttled) in 2028 to 2030.

The differences to explain. Against 2022 to 2025 (revenue growth of 12 to 15% a year, 27% compound over 2019 to 2024; adjusted EBITA growth of 17 to 38%; margin from about 8% to 9.5%; operating cash conversion from about 101% to 83% of adjusted EBITA; capex from 1% to 3.5% of sales; leverage rising to 2.37x), the forecast has revenue growth of 15.3% in 2026 falling to 10.3%, 9.5%, 9.0% and 8.5%; adjusted EBITA growth of 19%, 12.5%, 14%, 11% and 9.5%; the margin to 10.7% by 2030; free cash flow conversion (model convention) rising from about 20% of adjusted EBITA in 2026 to about 50% by 2028; and leverage falling.

Driver by driver, with weights. (1) The acquired contribution to sales growth falls from 12 to 13% (H1 2026) to 10.5% in 2026, 7% in 2027, 6% in 2028 and 4.5% by 2030. This one driver accounts for about 70% of the revenue deceleration and about 60% of the EBITA deceleration between history and forecast; it is a function of the deal budget (SEK 0.95bn to 1.05bn of new deals a year), which is a function of free cash flow after dividends, which is a function of cash conversion. Everything the second set wrote about the feedback loops reduces to this chain. (2) Currency: a 3% a year headwind in 2024 and 2025 becomes -0.5%, -1.5%, -0.5% and nil; it is external, unforecastable beyond a year, and helps the forecast relative to history. (3) The margin's source changes: about 80 basis points of cumulative help from provisioning over 2023 to 2025 (DNB Carnegie's figures) ends and partly reverses (-SEK 15m in Q4 2026, -SEK 25m a year from 2027), while Gothenburg (+SEK 55m to 60m a year from 2028, about 30 basis points), pruning (+10 to 15 basis points a year) and acquired mix (+20 to 30) arrive; the net annual gain is unchanged at about 30 basis points, so the margin difference is small and its drivers are five or six items of 10 to 30 basis points each, dated and mostly inside management's control. Diffuse. (4) Below EBITA: the tax rate's fall from 33% to 24% already happened in 2025; net financial items stop rising (they rose from SEK 179m to 407m over 2022 to 2024) but, after my change, do not fall; depreciation rises with the estate. Concentrated in the interest line, which depends on net debt, which depends on (1). (5) Cash: the forecast's tripling of free cash flow from 2026 to 2028 comes from the Gothenburg capex tail ending (about +SEK 300m against 2025), earn-outs falling from SEK 463m to SEK 150m to 250m (+SEK 200m to 300m against 2026), and inventory days falling from 82 to 72 (+SEK 150m to 200m a year). The first two are dated and guided; the third is behavioural and undemonstrated. Concentrated in three items, two of them mechanical.

How often forecasts resting on one driver were met in the cohort. The AlphaSense report's "five comparable past cases" is more assertion than data, but the pattern in the files is clear enough. At self-funded compounders (Lagercrantz, Lifco, Addtech, Indutrade) forecasts resting on the deal engine were met for a decade because the engine never depended on a recovery in anything; at levered acquirers (AddLife 2022 to 2023, Storskogen 2022 to 2024, Vimian 2022) forecasts resting on continued acquisitions were missed by 15 to 30% on EBITA over the following two years because the engine stopped. Asker sits between: its 2027 engine is two-thirds fuelled already (D-02); its 2028 to 2030 engine is not.

What this means for the memo. Present the case as having one swing variable and give its three states, as the second set did: free cash flow conversion at 45% of adjusted EBITA (deal budget SEK 0.8bn, acquired growth 5%, leverage flat at 2.3 to 2.4x, 2028 EBITA about SEK 2.3bn); 55% (base, SEK 2.43bn); 70% (budget SEK 1.5bn, acquired growth 9 to 10%, 2028 EBITA about SEK 2.55bn). Across the three the 2028 EBITA cone is about plus or minus 6% and the 2028 EPS cone plus or minus 8%; for 2027 the cone is half that because the acquired contribution is largely in hand. A concentrated driver is also a monitorable one: the four indicators that decide it are all disclosed quarterly (inventory days at each report; statutory operating cash flow for FY2026 against about SEK 1.3bn before earn-outs; the run-rate of announced deals at SEK 200m to 250m a quarter; reported leverage at or below 2.2x at end-2027).

D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed, and so how predictable is the case?

❌ D48

Question asked How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

Judgement: the cone is narrow for revenue, moderate for adjusted EBITA, moderate-to-wide for EPS and wide for free cash flow; it is skewed slightly negative at the EBITA and EPS lines and clearly negative at the cash line; and the cone for the share price is wider than the cone for any operating line because the price is set by a disclosure dispute rather than by the operating outcome. The case is therefore predictable at the operating level and unpredictable at the equity level until the FY2026 accounts are published. That is the honest summary for the memo.

The cones, line by line, for 2027 (the model's 18-month column ends 31 December 2027). Revenue: SEK 20.9bn to 21.8bn around 21.35bn (plus or minus 2%), symmetric; the only external variable of size is the krona (a 5% move is about SEK 750m of reported sales) and the only internal one is deal timing. Adjusted EBITA: SEK 2,000m to 2,250m around 2,134m (about -6% to +5%); I widen the second set's lower bound from 2,020m to admit a 10 to 20% probability of a disclosed one-off provisioning and capitalisation catch-up of SEK 100m to 150m in the FY2026 or FY2027 accounts. The downside items are more numerous and more likely to be larger than smaller: Gothenburg slipping one quarter (40% probability, SEK 10m to 15m) or two (15%, SEK 20m to 30m); the catch-up; a device-line loss to an OEM going direct (10 to 15% a year, 20 to 40 basis points of margin); a Dutch insurer re-contracting away (under 5%, several hundred million of sales). The upside items are fewer and lumpier: a deal pace at the framework's 10 to 12% (which cash does not fund without leverage rising); a Nordic preparedness order cycle worth 1 to 2 points of organic growth in the year it lands (the 2024 round was SEK 350m; Sykehusinnkjøp, NESA's 2024 to 2028 purchasing system and Swedish regional tenders are visible only in fragments); a krona that stays weak, worth SEK 10m to 15m a half. EPS on the model's basis: SEK 3.20 to 3.80 around 3.55 (-10% to +7%), negative skew, because interest and depreciation can only disappoint from here and a clean-up would take a one-off bite; on a cash basis SEK 2.80 to 3.30 around 3.05. Free cash flow before acquisitions: SEK 700m to 1,300m around the model's 950m (plus or minus 30%), negative skew, on inventory (each five days is about SEK 220m), earn-out timing and the tax convention. Leverage at end-2027 on the company's definition: 1.95x to 2.45x around 2.2x.

Why the cone has this shape. The second set's sensitivities hold: 50 basis points of margin is worth about SEK 105m of 2027 EBITA (5%), a point of organic growth about SEK 20m to 25m (1%), so the EBITA cone is a margin cone, and the margin's drivers are mostly internal and dated (Gothenburg, provisioning, mix) with one external tail (OEM reclamation). Demand contributes almost nothing to the width: healthcare volumes did not fall in 2008 to 2010 at McKesson or Cardinal Health, Oriola's one lost quarter in 2020 reversed within a quarter, and AddLife's 110 basis point sequential margin fall in Q3 2026 on Spanish strikes shows that downside gearing exists but is small and transient. The cash cone is wide because three items (inventory, earn-outs, tax) each move SEK 100m to 200m and none is correlated with the EBITA line.

The historical dispersion at comparable acquirers, as a check. Over 18-month windows the cohort splits cleanly. Self-funded compounders show narrow cones: Lagercrantz's fifteen-year EPS streak ended on a 3 to 5% EBITA miss in Q1 2026/27 and the multiple fell from 23x; Lifco fell 16% on a short report in January 2026 with no change in its numbers. Levered acquirers show wide cones with a fat downside: AddLife missed its 2022 to 2023 expectations by a wide margin as leverage reached 3.5 to 3.8x, paused acquisitions for two years and de-rated to a 26% discount; Storskogen's forecasts were cut by half over 2022 to 2024; Uniphar's leverage went from 1.6x to 2.4x in one half-year (H1 2026) on a working-capital unwind, which shows how fast a distributor's ratio can move. The skew events in the sector have been one-sided: distribution-centre programmes slipped at Uniphar (go-live deferred to February 2027, EUR 20m of extra capital) and Oriola (programme pushed from 2027 to 2028, EUR 54m of extra cost, a target-price cut) and never arrived early; OEM reclamation took SEK 140m of AddLife's annual sales at a stroke; earnings-quality critiques produced de-ratings (Lifco -16%, Asker -20% against AddLife) and, where provisions were rebuilt (Storskogen's SEK 65m of "financial tidying" in 2024), one-off hits before any recovery. The only recurring upside skew event in the files is the preparedness cycle, which is lumpy, external and slow, and which the first set rightly left out of the base case.

The share price cone, which is what Richard will feel. The first set's fair trough (SEK 45 to 50) and upside case (SEK 95 to 105) still stand; the model's 18-month value (SEK 111) sits above the upside case because it applies the pre-dispute median multiple. Around SEK 71 the distribution is roughly symmetric in distance and asymmetric in probability: the path to the trough needs one adverse disclosure event (an auditor-driven or management-initiated restatement, 10 to 20%); the path to the upside needs four to six quarters of proof. For twelve months I put the probability-weighted centre near SEK 75 to 85 and the modal path as a range-bound stock until February 2027 and a re-rating thereafter that is conditional on the accounts.

Predictability overall: moderate. The operating forecasts for the next 18 months are more predictable than the market's treatment of the stock implies (revenue plus or minus 2%, adjusted EBITA -6% to +5%), and the equity outcome is less predictable than the operating forecasts because it depends on a document not yet written. For a memo that must judge the predictability of the financial forecasts, the answer is: good on the lines the company guides, poor on the lines it does not, and the market prices the second set of lines.

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure for monitoring the case over the next six months?

C54

Question asked How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

Judgement: good for the operating drivers (organic growth and adjusted EBITA by business area, deal flow with sales per deal, the two leverage ratios, the contingent-consideration balance and payments, the rolling 12-month margin and growth); adequate but requiring correction for cash (statutory operating cash flow, inventory and net working capital are disclosed quarterly, but the headline "adjusted" operating cash flow adds back earn-outs and must be reversed, SEK 238m in Q2 2026); and poor for the three things the market is actually arguing about (capitalised development, provisioning levels and useful lives appear only in the annual report; the Gothenburg double-running cost is described but not quantified by quarter; the contributors to the company-compiled consensus are not named). Over the next six months, which contain the Q3 2026 report (4 November), the Q4 and full-year report (early February 2027) and possibly the FY2026 annual report (February to April 2027), the Consumer can track four of the six deciding drivers quarterly and must wait for the annual report for the other two.

What each report lets the Consumer see, and what it does not. Q3 2026 (4 November): organic sales growth by business area against DNB Carnegie's 5% (the comparison is a normal 5% in Q3 2025); adjusted EBITA against SEK 456m (19%) and the North margin against 13.1% to 13.6%, which is where double manning shows; acquired growth and the deals closed (Murray Surgical from July; Ambercell, Labo Clinic and Med Kjemi in Q3 or Q4); the two leverage ratios, which I expect at 2.35x to 2.45x reported and 2.45x to 2.55x on the alternative definition including earn-outs due within twelve months, so the alternative ratio may print at or just above the 2.5x target for the first time (about 25%), a narrative event even though the covenant is 3.50x; statutory operating cash flow and inventory, where Asker has no seasonal collection and a soft print is likely; any statement on Q4 2026 and Q1 2027 acquisitions. Not visible: Gothenburg cost in kronor; capitalised development; provisions. Q4 and FY2026 (February 2027): the first full-year statutory cash flow since the critique (the test is about SEK 1.3bn before earn-outs); year-end inventory days (below 80 is the first good sign); the proposed dividend (consensus SEK 0.725, the model SEK 0.70); the rolling 12-month margin (9.8 to 9.9%, not yet across 10%); year-end leverage (2.2 to 2.3x reported); 2027 earn-out guidance (SEK 130m to 150m) and acquisitions signed. Not visible until the annual report: the obsolescence reserve as a share of gross inventory (6.5% in 2025, from 14.9% in 2021); credit-loss provisions against receivables overdue by more than two months (up 170% in 2025); capitalised development (SEK 94m in 2025) and distribution rights; the software useful life (ten years, against three to five at AddLife and Lifco and three to seven at Lagercrantz); any change in the leverage definition; any internal audit function. The AGM in May 2027 may carry a revised LTIP (the 2026 plan vests on a 12.5% EBITA floor).

How helpful, driver by driver. Cash conversion and inventory: monitorable quarterly and the single most useful disclosure, provided the Consumer reads the company's own cash-flow statement rather than FactSet's half-yearly derivation and subtracts the earn-outs the company adds back. Deal pace: fully monitorable (each deal is announced with sales and employees; EBITA is given only for the half-year cohort, SEK 47m on SEK 273m of sales in H1 2026). Leverage: fully monitorable on both company definitions, and the bank's definition (pro forma EBITDA; leases and long-term earn-outs excluded; earn-outs due within twelve months included) is now understood from the prospectus and the Q1 2026 call, so covenant headroom can be computed. Gothenburg: only through North's margin and the CEO's qualitative statements; the savings of SEK 50m to 60m from H2 2027 will be visible only as a North margin step in Q3 and Q4 2027. Accounting quality: annual only, so the six-month window gives one observation at most. Organic momentum: monitorable by business area, with no price-volume split.

What the company could disclose that would most raise this score, in order of value to the memo: a quarterly figure for Gothenburg double-running cost; the obsolescence reserve and credit-loss provision in the interim reports; a leverage ratio including all contingent consideration (AddLife's practice); capitalised development each quarter; and the names of the consensus contributors. None costs the company anything but the option of presenting itself at its most favourable, and the new Chair's first annual report is the natural moment. Until then the Consumer monitors the operating story well and the accounting story once a year, and the accounting story is the one that moves the stock.

D-06

How clearly does what the company says trace to drivers, lines and EPS, and how clear is the external transmission?

❌ D47

Question asked How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

Judgement: the trace from what the company says to adjusted EBITA is clear and quantified; the trace from adjusted EBITA to EPS and cash is reconstructible from the accounts but is not narrated by the company and is where every disagreement in the market lives; and the external trace is clear for the two financial transmissions (rates and currency) and weak for the three value-chain transmissions that matter (tender pricing, OEM channel choice and Dutch insurer contracting). The company's communication makes the first half of the earnings bridge predictable and leaves the second half to the analyst.

The internal trace, from statement to line. Management states: organic adjusted EBITA growth of 5 to 8%; acquisitions contributing 10 to 12% of EBITA growth, at 6 to 8x, 10 to 20 a year; a margin above 10% from mix and scale; leverage below 2.5x; return on net working capital above 50%; a dividend above 30%; Gothenburg savings of SEK 50m to 60m from H2 2027 with double manning in late 2026 and early 2027 and about SEK 150m of 2026 capital; about SEK 150m of 2027 earn-outs; organic sales slightly above a 3 to 4% market. From these the Consumer can build organic sales of 4.5 to 5% (market plus 1 to 2 points, demonstrated), organic EBITA of 6 to 7% through a 12 to 14% incremental margin (demonstrated in H1 2026), an acquired contribution of 7% in the model against 10 to 12% stated (the one place the model departs from the company, on cash grounds), and a margin path from the business-area margins (North 13.6%, West 9.6%, Central 9.2%, with the Gothenburg step landing in North and the pruning in Central and West), and so adjusted EBITA of SEK 1,896m in 2026 and 2,134m in 2027 within a few per cent. The milestones are identifiable and dated: rolling 12-month adjusted EBITA growth above 15% (met now; below 15% from the Q2 2027 report on the model); the rolling margin above 10% (FY2027 report); North's margin stepping to about 13.8% in H2 2027; reported leverage at or below 2.2x at end-2027; the alternative ratio below 2.5x throughout.

Where the trace breaks. Below adjusted EBITA the company guides nothing. Amortisation of acquired intangibles (about SEK 341m in 2025, about 400m in 2026) is disclosed but its path depends on the deal pace and the purchase-price allocations; items affecting comparability (SEK 204m in 2025, SEK 9m in H1 2026) are itemised but their recurring component (DNB Carnegie's SEK 15m a quarter) is a judgement; depreciation other than acquired intangibles (SEK 421m in 2025) is disclosed in total with capitalised development's amortisation inside it; net financial items (SEK 350m in 2025) are disclosed with a rate sensitivity (SEK 35m per percentage point) but no guidance; the tax rate (22.6% in 2025) is not guided and the deferred-tax treatment of acquired intangibles is not explained, which is why the cross-check gap in the model exists; the share count is stable. The result is the fourfold EPS definition problem the second set laid out and a FactSet consensus that blends three brokers on three bases. The company's building blocks describe how sales become adjusted EBITA; below that, in the first set's phrase, the earnings "just come about", and the company's own adjusted EPS (SEK 2.70 for 2025, adding back amortisation and items affecting comparability without tax) is the least conservative of the four bases.

The external trace, actor by actor. Public buyers: like-for-like tender prices fall 2 to 3% a year and 55% of European tenders are decided mainly on price; this reaches the gross margin and is offset by mix; it is not traceable because there is no price-volume bridge, so the Consumer sees only the net (gross margin 41.1% in 2025 to about 42%). German reimbursement caps for 2027 to 2029 reach Central's margin; not quantified by the company or any broker. OEMs: a decision to go direct in a device line above about SEK 20m to 30m of local sales removes sales and gross margin at once (AddLife's SEK 140m); not traceable before the event, and because the company reports geographically rather than by product the exposure cannot be mapped (the OEM report could identify only acquired subsidiaries above the threshold, not lines). Dutch insurers: five payers, one-year framework contracts with extension options, no public tariff history; the transmission to West's organic growth and margin is real and invisible; the research commissioned on it found no evidence of re-pricing since 2023 and no 2027 tariff publication yet. Rates: SEK 35m per percentage point, about SEK 0.07 of EPS; clear. Currency: 69% of revenue in euros, translation unhedged, a 1% move worth about SEK 3.7m of earnings; clear. Preparedness orders: SEK 350m in 2024 shows the scale; the 2027 to 2029 pipeline is visible only in fragments and lands as a surprise. Regulation (MDR and its simplification): reaches the target pipeline over years; untraceable in the numbers.

Implication for the memo. The Consumer can state the EBITA milestones with confidence and must define the EPS basis in every sentence that uses one; the external risks that could move EPS by 5% or more in a quarter (an OEM reclaim, a Dutch insurer loss) are the ones with no transmission visible in advance, and the memo should say so rather than pretend to trace them.

D-07

Does the share price and multiple move with peers or idiosyncratically, and so is the multiple company-driven?

C56

Question asked To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (on a rolling six-month basis), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

Judgement: the share price and the multiple have moved idiosyncratically, not with the sector, over the last six months and for most of the stock's nineteen months of listed life; the multiple is company-driven, and the thing driving it is the earnings-quality dispute. For the fair-multiple question this means the stock's own history is the relevant anchor and the peer basket is not, but the own history is short and most of it was set at the pre-dispute premium, so the median of that history is a bull-case multiple for the next 18 months rather than a neutral one.

The co-movement numbers. Over the latest 126-day window (28 April to 23 October 2026) the correlation of Asker's daily returns with the equal-weighted basket of its dashboard peers is 0.33, the beta 0.90 and the R-squared 11%: 89% of the stock's daily variance is its own. The stock returned +0.3% over the window while the basket returned +20.7%. No pairwise correlation exceeds 0.26 (Apotea and Fielmann), and the two peers closest in business (Uniphar 0.15, Galenica 0.10) are among the lowest. Over the 24 month-end windows the R-squared was 32% in the window ending April 2025 (which contains only about five weeks of listed trading and is not meaningful), 8 to 14% through 2025, 2 to 4% in the first quarter of 2026 and 6 to 14% since; the beta rose from about 0.4 in early 2026 to 0.9 to 1.0 now, so the stock has become more sensitive to sector moves in direction while remaining almost entirely idiosyncratic in variance. Two cautions on the basket: it is not a true peer set (two pharmacy retailers, an optician, a hearing-aid retailer, a Swiss wholesaler, an Irish distributor), and AddLife, the real comparator, has no price history in the file. Against AddLife the relevant fact is the 20% relative underperformance between 10 September and 1 October 2026 that DNB Carnegie cited for its upgrade: the idiosyncratic event itself.

The multiple. Asker's 24-month-forward P/E is 19.5x at the 9th percentile of its own range (median 24.0x, range 16.7x to 34.7x over 76 weekly observations, which is the listed period, not five years); its trailing P/E of 19.7x is at the 12th percentile of the history FactSet constructs. AddLife, in the same dashboard sector and the same business, trades at 29.4x trailing at the 91st percentile of its history. Two companies with, on DNB Carnegie's own like-for-like figures, nearly identical lease-inclusive leverage (2.8 to 2.9x) and adjusted EBITA over working capital sit at opposite ends of their ranges because one has three years of proven deleveraging and clean accounts behind it and the other has a forensic note. That is as clear a demonstration of a company-driven multiple as the data allow. Sector flows have not helped: healthcare has been bought as a destination for money leaving technology (Jefferies), the basket is up 20% in six months, and Asker's relative strength percentile is 20 with the 200-day average falling in every one of the last twelve months.

What this means for predictability and for the fair multiple. For the forecasts' predictability it is mildly positive: the evidence that will decide the multiple is company evidence the Consumer can monitor (D-05), not a sector rotation nobody can forecast, and a sector de-rating would not take the stock down much because the stock does not move with the sector. For the fair multiple (the memo's topic #11) three observations. First, the model's 18-month return of +55% has 45 points from the multiple moving from 18.5x to about 24x, the median of the listed history; because that history is nineteen months long and most of it was at a premium rating, the median is not the mid-point of a cycle but the pre-dispute level, and regaining it within 18 months requires clean FY2026 accounts and a strong FY2026 cash flow, which is the upside case (first set: SEK 95 to 105), not the base. Second, the phase (topic #10) is bottoming rather than turning up: technical momentum F, revisions C, the 52-week low of SEK 56.2 set after the downgrade not retested, no catalyst before 4 November, and a proof cycle whose first real test is in February 2027; the Viewforth guideline puts a bottoming or turning stock in the lower part of its range, which for Asker is 17 to 21x. Third, on the model's 2029 EPS of SEK 4.70 (the 24-month-forward figure at December 2027) and SEK 1.55 of dividends, the 18-month return is about +14% at 17x, +23% at 18.5x (no re-rating), +33% at 20x and +43% at 21.5x, against the model's +55% at the median; the memo should present the model's figure as the return if the proof cycle succeeds in full and the lower figures as the base and intermediate cases. The multiple is company-driven; the company has not yet done the things that would drive it up.

Risks

C540 questions

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The third set's answer is that Asker's financial forecasts for the next 18 months are predictable at the operating level and unpredictable at the equity level, and that the two are separated by one document, the FY2026 annual report. The earlier sets cohere around one spine (cash conversion to leverage to deal pace to EBITA growth) and their differences are small and resolved. The forecast rests about two-thirds to 70% on demonstrated strengths and completed actions for 2027 and 55 to 60% for 2028 to 2030, with the undemonstrated part being inventory discipline and cash conversion at the enlarged scale rather than any capability the company lacks in kind; with the deal engine fully stopped from today, 2027 adjusted EBITA growth would still be 8 to 9%. The drivers of the difference from history are concentrated in one monitorable variable for growth and diffuse for margin. The cone is narrow for revenue (plus or minus 2%), moderate for adjusted EBITA (-6% to +5%), moderate-to-wide for EPS (-10% to +7%) and wide for cash (plus or minus 30%), with a negative skew that comes from dated internal items (Gothenburg, provisioning) and one external tail (OEM reclamation). Disclosure lets the Consumer monitor four of the six deciding drivers quarterly and the two accounting ones annually. The earnings bridge is traceable to adjusted EBITA and not below. The multiple is company-driven, so it will be moved by the company's own disclosures and not by the sector. The model's own cash flow is lower than the second set's cross-check by SEK 105m to 135m a year because of the tax convention, which leaves net debt drifting up rather than flat and the leverage path about 0.1 to 0.3 turns higher by 2028 to 2030; I change the net financial items input to match and change nothing else.

The steer for the memo. Build topic #9 (riskiness and simplicity) on the proposition that the case is simple and its risk is concentrated: one variable, three dated tests (the FY2026 cash flow and inventory count in February 2027; the FY2026 accounts' provisioning and capitalisation in February to April 2027; Gothenburg savings in North's margin in Q3 2027), and a 2027 EBITA that is largely secured whatever those tests show. Build topic #6 (forecast financials) on the model's figures with the free-cash-flow range stated, and present 2028 to 2030 as conditional on the cash recovery. Build topic #10 (lifecycle) as bottoming, not yet turning up, with the turn dated to February to April 2027 and conditional. The predictability score for the set is a little above neutral because the operating lines are more predictable than the market's treatment of the stock implies and the cash line is less predictable than the company's presentation implies, and the market prices the cash line.

Layer 4: Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

The Investment Analyst's heading The fourth set: guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative

The fourth set as a whole C52 written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The gap between our assessment and what guidance, consensus and the narrative reflect is unfavourable on the lines the company and the sell-side use to measure themselves and favourable on the lines the market actually prices. The company's framework (15% adjusted EBITA growth) will be met in 2026 and seen to be missed from July 2027; there is no conservative guidance to be raised and no beat-and-raise habit; the margin target is reached only at the edge of the window. The three-broker consensus has EBITDA about right, EPS growth and free cash flow too high for 2027 on a like-for-like basis and 2028 EBITDA too low, so the revision pattern of the last twelve months (EBITDA up, EPS and cash down, ratings down) continues for two more quarters. The narrative is a single forensic note, already softened to "balanced" by its author, that has priced the stock for a 20 to 25% overstatement of earnings and a forced halt to acquisitions, neither of which our assessment expects. What the narrative does not contain is the structure of the next 18 months: that about two-thirds to 70% of 2027 EBITA growth is secured and the floor with the engine stopped is 8 to 9%; that the covenant leaves more than a turn of headroom so any slowdown is chosen; that the first cash test (February 2027) will look weak for a structural reason; and that the document that decides the stock is the FY2026 annual report, with about a 60% probability of an outcome (gradual tightening or a disclosed mild clean-up) the price would receive well within a quarter. The two unarticulated downside dynamics (Dutch insurer concentration; founder retention) are real, undated and unmonitorable, and the memo should name them as the risks that would arrive without warning.

For topics #7, #8 and #10 to #12. Scope for upgrades over the coming 6 to 12 months exists in the narrative and the rating mix (one clean document moves a three-broker mix by a third) and not in the estimates, which are cut first. Momentum in guidance, revisions, narrative and price is currently negative or absent at Asker and positive at the compounder cohort, which widens the relative gap. The lifecycle phase is bottoming, with the turn dated to February to April 2027 and conditional on the accounts. The fair multiple over 18 months belongs in the lower part of the stock's range (17 to 21x) rather than at its median (24x), because the median is the pre-dispute premium and the proof cycle has not yet produced a data point; the model's 18-month return of +55% should be presented as the return if the proof cycle succeeds in full, with about +23% (no re-rating at 18.5x), +33% (20x) and +43% (21.5x) as the base and intermediate cases, and +14% at 17x as the case in which the accounts disappoint mildly and the engine slows. The net gap is modestly favourable: the operating case is better than the narrative, the cash case is worse than the consensus, and the price reflects the narrative.

Guidance

1 question
I-0144
I-01

How likely is guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next earnings events, and what would drive each outcome?

❌ D44

Question asked How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

Judgement: Asker gives no numerical guidance, so "raised, met or reduced" applies to its five standing targets and two pieces of operational guidance. Over the next 18 months: the adjusted EBITA growth target (above 15% a year) will be met for FY2026 (about 19%; probability above 90%) and missed for FY2027 on the model (12.5%; the probability of meeting 15% is 25 to 30%, which requires an acquired contribution of 9% or more and no clean-up charge), with the rolling 12-month growth rate falling below 15% at the Q2 2027 report in July 2027 (to about 12.6% on the model's quarters), which is when the market will register the miss; the margin target (above 10% over the medium term) will be touched on the rolling definition only at the FY2027 report in February 2028 (9.85% after Q3 2027) and never on a cash definition; the leverage target (below 2.5x) will be met on the company's definition throughout (90%) and may be printed at or above 2.5x on the company's own alternative definition at Q3 2026 (about 25%), which would be read as a breach of the company's own target; the return on net working capital and dividend targets will be met. No target will be raised in the period (probability under 10% for each). The probability of a formal reduction of the growth target is low (10 to 15%), because the company will present 2027 as a year of digestion within a medium-term framework; the more likely change (30 to 40%) is a re-presentation under the new Chair: a leverage ratio including all contingent consideration, a cash-flow or cash-conversion target, and possibly a first capital markets day. For 18 months to three years out, the framework's 15% is met in 2028 on the model (14%) only if cash conversion recovers, and missed in 2029 to 2030 (11%, 9.5%); I put the probability that the growth target is lowered or reframed by the end of 2028 at 40% and raised at under 5%.

Event by event. Q3 2026 (4 November 2026): no guidance to move; what matters is adjusted EBITA against DNB Carnegie's SEK 456m and the company-compiled consensus (I expect SEK 450m to 470m, 18 to 22% growth, with the record of four beats of 4 to 7% in six quarters favouring the upper half), organic growth at 4 to 6% against a normal comparison, North's margin down 50 to 100 basis points year on year on double manning, acquired growth of about 10 points, the alternative leverage ratio near 2.5x, and statutory operating cash flow that will look weak against EBITA because Asker has no third-quarter collection either. Drivers of a better print: Ambercell and Med Kjemi consolidating from September; the krona's weakness since September adding SEK 5m to 10m. Of a worse one: Gothenburg costs above "low tens of millions"; a contract loss; an earn-out revaluation charge inside items affecting comparability (non-cash, excluded from adjusted EBITA, visible in reported EBIT). Q4 and FY2026 (early February 2027): the "met" on growth (19%) and leverage (2.2 to 2.3x), the "not yet" on margin (rolling 9.8 to 9.9%), the proposed dividend (SEK 0.70 to 0.73), the first full-year cash flow (the test is SEK 1.3bn before earn-outs; I put 50% on passing it), inventory days (below 80 is a pass; 45%), and 2027 earn-out guidance (SEK 130m to 150m; met). FY2026 annual report (February to April 2027): the document that decides the fourth set. Outcomes and my probabilities: no change in policies and no added disclosure (35%; the discount persists); gradual tightening with added disclosure and a modest provision rebuild of SEK 30m to 60m (40%; read positively after a day); a larger one-off of SEK 100m to 150m with a leverage re-definition (20%; a one-off hit of 5 to 7 points to 2027 adjusted EBITA growth, read positively within a quarter because the narrative already assumes worse); an auditor-driven restatement (5%; the severe case). AGM (May 2027): a possible LTIP tilt toward cash metrics; no guidance. Q1 2027 (May): margin 9.7%, growth about 12%, the last double-manning quarter. Q2 2027 (July): rolling growth falls to about 12.6%; the first "below 15%" headline; rolling margin 9.76%. Q3 2027 (November 2027): Gothenburg savings visible in North (about 13.8%); rolling growth about 12%; the quarter in which the operating story is strongest and the framework most visibly missed. Q4 2027 (February 2028): the rolling margin touches 10.0%; leverage at or below 2.2x on the model, 2.0x if the second set's cash flow proves right; the year's growth 12 to 13% against 15%.

What would drive a raise, a meet or a cut of the framework. A raise would need the margin to cross 10% with a clean annual report and cash conversion above 60%, and would most likely be a new margin ambition (11 to 12% by 2030) at a capital markets day rather than a change to the growth figure; probability 15% within 18 months. A meet in spirit (12 to 14% growth presented as "in line with the framework over time", the margin above 10%, leverage below 2.5x) is the modal outcome. A cut would need either a chosen slowdown made explicit (a stated deal budget below SEK 1bn) or the severe accounting case; 10 to 15%.

How favourable the gap is. Our assessment is below the growth framework from 2027 and in line with the margin and leverage targets; the sell-side already models 7% acquired growth and 10% EBITDA growth for 2027, so the framework miss is priced in the numbers if not yet in the narrative; and the one piece of operational guidance that could surprise positively, the Gothenburg savings, is more likely to arrive late than early. The gap is neutral to slightly unfavourable: there is no conservative guidance to be raised, there is a framework that will be seen to be missed in mid-2027, and the compensations are a leverage target held and a margin target reached at the edge of the window. The management behaviour that would change this score is the one C-70 found absent: a beat-and-raise habit.

Sell-side estimates

1 question
I-0246
I-02

How likely are consensus forecasts and the net breadth of revisions to move higher or lower, and why?

❌ D46

Question asked How likely are sell-side consensus forecasts, and the net breadth of revisions, to move higher or lower over the next few earnings events (over the next 18 months): to be upgraded, cut or held, and why? Secondarily, how likely are the consensus forecasts for 18 months to three years out to be upgraded, cut or held?

Judgement: over the next 18 months the sell-side consensus is more likely to be cut than raised on EPS (2026 and 2027) and free cash flow (2027), held on revenue, cut by 1 to 3% on 2026 EBITDA and held on 2027 EBITDA, and raised on 2028 EBITDA. The net breadth of revisions stays negative to neutral through the Q3 and Q4 2026 reports and turns positive only if the FY2026 accounts and cash flow pass their tests, in which case the Q2 and Q3 2027 reports (rolling growth falling but Gothenburg savings arriving) carry the upgrades. The pattern of the last twelve months (FY2027 EBITDA +11%, FY2027 EPS -3%, FY2028 EPS -14%, target price -8% over three months, buy share -17 points) continues for two more quarters and then either reverses or hardens. The single event most likely to move the breadth and the Hold-heavy rating mix is the FY2026 annual report, not the Q4 cash flow and not the Q3 2027 savings, because the ratings dispute is about accounting and only the accounts can settle it.

Where consensus sits against the model, line by line, and what that implies. Revenue: consensus SEK 19,262m, 21,214m and 22,798m against the model's 19,355m, 21,349m and 23,377m (+0.5%, +0.6%, +2.5%); held through 2027; 2028 drifts up as DNB Carnegie's 4% growth assumption proves low. EBITDA (brokers' basis): consensus 2,436m, 2,684m and 2,838m against 2,381m, 2,669m and 3,016m (-2.3%, -0.6%, +6.3%); 2026 is cut 1 to 3% after Q3 and Q4 on Gothenburg and year-end prudence; 2027 is held in level while its growth rate rises from 10% toward 12% because the base falls; 2028 is raised over 2027 as deals continue, unless cash conversion fails, in which case DNB Carnegie's 2,753m is right. Depreciation and amortisation: consensus flat at about SEK 500m against the model's 535m and 585m; raised as a cost by SEK 30m to 60m a year. Net financial items: consensus implies about SEK 300m to 316m flat; the model, after my change, has 330m, 350m and 355m; DNB Carnegie already has 336m and 372m; the Nordea and restricted figures are too low and will be raised as a cost. Tax: 23.5% against 24%. EPS: the median SEK 2.79, 3.29 and 3.77 blends a cash-basis broker (Nordea 2.47, 3.13, 3.75), an add-back broker (DNB Carnegie 3.57, 3.81, 4.01) and one between (3.05, 3.70, 4.29); the model's basis prints 3.11, 3.55 and 4.15, about 10% above the median by definition and not a view. The model's 2026 EPS growth of +30% is against FactSet's Viewforth-definition 2025 actual (SEK 2.38), not the second set's SEK 2.51; the like-for-like growth comparison is 23%, 14%, 17% for the model against consensus 21%, 18%, 15%, so 2027 EPS growth is too high by about four points on interest and depreciation and will be cut 3 to 5%, and 2028 is about right in growth and too low in level if EBITDA is raised. Free cash flow: consensus SEK 805m, 1,262m and 1,388m on a definition that deducts fixed-asset capex of SEK 285m to 403m and leases but not capitalised development; the model's 377m, 950m and 1,228m deduct SEK 520m, 435m and 435m of capex including SEK 180m to 220m of intangibles, and tax on adjusted pre-tax profit; like for like, consensus 2027 free cash flow is 15 to 25% too high and will be cut when the FY2026 cash flow is reported and when the annual report publishes capitalised development. Dividend: in line.

Why the breadth stays negative near term. Three of the next four prints (Q3 2026 cash, Q4 2026 cash and inventory, the FY2026 capitalised-development disclosure) land on lines where consensus is too high, and the one line on which consensus is about right or too low (adjusted EBITA) is the line the market has stopped paying for. The mechanics of a three-broker consensus are also lumpy: DNB Carnegie supplies the forensic adjustments and has already made them (its 2027 EBITDA of 2,650m is the low end; its free cash flow of 1,159m is below the median), so the cuts to come are Nordea's and the restricted broker's if they adopt any of the cash adjustments, and Nordea's Buy at SEK 106 (7 October 2026) is the rating most at risk of moving to Hold (25 to 30% within six months). The upgrade most likely within 18 months is DNB Carnegie's return to Buy (35 to 40%, after a clean annual report), since its own October note frames the risk-reward as balanced and its 15x EV to cash EBITA valuation would move with any evidence that cash EBITA is higher than its SEK 1.2bn reconstruction. The dashboard's revision-momentum score of 3 (C) and the buy share of 67% describe a stock mid-way through a cycle, not at either end.

The read-across from peers. AddLife's Q3 2026 beat (EPS SEK 1.05 against 0.86) was met with a downgrade to Hold because the run was priced; its Q4 2026 cash collection (3 February 2027) will set the template the market applies to Asker's Q4 a week later, and if AddLife collects as it did in Q4 2025 (SEK 888m) while Asker's working capital rises again, the contrast will be drawn even though the second set showed Asker has no fourth-quarter release and the comparison is unfair. Lifco's, Addtech's and Indutrade's estimates are being raised on currency and cyclical recovery; the compounder cohort is in an upgrade cycle Asker is not part of, which widens the relative gap and makes any Asker upgrade more visible when it comes. Vimian's working-capital normalisation (25% of sales from 26%) and 71% cash conversion in Q3 2026 show what a recovering cash story earns: a resumed acquisition and a steadier rating.

The secondary question. The 2028 consensus (EBITDA SEK 2,838m on a range of 2,753m to 3,137m; EPS 3.77 on 3.75 to 4.29) is a guess by three brokers with incompatible definitions, and the FY2029 figures rest on one broker and should not be used. On the model, 2028 EBITDA is 6% above consensus and will be revised up gradually if deals continue, while 2028 EPS will be revised toward the cash basis as the Nordea convention spreads, so the pattern of the last year (EBITDA up, EPS down) continues into the out-years; the triggers are leverage printing below 2.0x (2029 on the model's cash flow) and a capital markets day, neither of which is dated. The memo should not treat "consensus below the framework" as conservatism: the sell-side's 7% acquired growth is a judgement about cash, not a sandbag.

How favourable the gap is. Our EBITDA is in line to above consensus and our EPS and cash are below on a like-for-like basis; the direction of the next two quarters' revisions is against the stock on the lines it trades on; the reversal is dated and conditional. Neutral to slightly unfavourable, with the proviso that a three-broker consensus with one Buy at SEK 106, one forensic Hold at SEK 66.5 and one Hold at SEK 82 is a setup in which a single clean document moves the mix by a third.

Stock market narrative

1 question
I-0360
I-03

How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in sell-side and investor commentary?

C60

Question asked How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of (i) sell-side commentary and (ii) investor commentary (for example on Twitter): how well known is each of them to investors who own or cover the company or its sector, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to three years out?

Judgement: the current narrative is the September 2026 earnings-quality narrative, nearly whole, and it is a one-broker narrative amplified by retail forums; the operating story (5% organic growth, 19 to 23% adjusted EBITA growth, a 10% margin in sight, 800 pipeline candidates) survives in two brokers' ratings and the company's own reports but has lost the floor. Of the fourteen dynamics tested, five are widely articulated (the dispute itself; the EPS-definition blend, in DNB Carnegie's note if not in investors' practice; the leverage-to-deal-pace loop, articulated with the wrong constraint; Gothenburg's dip-then-step, articulated without numbers; the depreciation and interest gap), four are partly articulated (the source of gross-margin gains; OEM bypass, in Carnegie's May 2025 initiation and one expert call; the new Chair as a clean-up agent, noted but not modelled; the preparedness cycle, as a 2025 headwind only), and five are articulated by no one who covers or owns the stock (the absence of a fourth-quarter working-capital release; the Dutch insurer concentration; founder retention after the 2022 to 2023 cohort's earn-outs; the MDR simplification's effect on the pipeline; and, outside the retail forums, the CFO's May 2026 share sale). Three points that this set and the second have established are in no commentary at all: that about two-thirds to 70% of 2027 EBITA growth is already secured; that the bank covenant's definition (leases and long-term earn-outs excluded, acquired EBITDA pro forma, 3.50x against an alternative ratio of 2.43x) makes a forced stop remote; and that the free-cash-flow consensus is too high for a definitional reason (capitalised development) rather than an operating one.

The sell-side narrative, house by house. DNB Carnegie (Hold, SEK 66.5, 1 October 2026; Sell, SEK 60, 10 September; Buy, SEK 105, 5 August) set the narrative: cash EBITA of SEK 1.2bn for 2025 against 1.6bn reported, a 7.3% cash margin, leverage of 3.1 to 3.4x all-in, SEK 60m of provisioning catch-up on each of inventory and receivables, 7% acquired growth from 2027, 15x EV to cash EBITA, and a risk-reward "balanced" after a 20% fall. Everything the AlphaSense reports written for this set say about Asker's narrative is drawn from these two documents and from Carnegie's May 2025 initiation; the research pool has one voice. Nordea (Buy, SEK 106, 7 October 2026) and the restricted broker (Hold, SEK 82, 20 August) have published nothing in the files that engages with the forensic case; their estimates (Nordea's 2026 EPS of SEK 2.47 on a cash basis; the restricted broker's 3.05) show they were already on more conservative definitions than DNB Carnegie's pre-September 3.57, which is the irony of the dispute: the broker that changed its mind had been the least conservative. Redeye's AddLife work (18 September 2026) frames Asker as the larger, lower-margin, less proven comparator and treats return on working capital (67% against 62%) as the one line where Asker is not behind. No house articulates the proof-cycle timetable as this set does, and none has written that the Q4 cash print will look weak for a structural reason that is not a deterioration.

The investor narrative. The files reach it only second-hand (Avanza forum discussions in September 2026, cited through DNB Carnegie; the CFO's share sale as a retail anxiety; no Twitter, X, podcast or fund-letter material surfaced, which is a gap in the research rather than evidence of silence, and I request it as OWN-3). What can be inferred: the free float is about half the shares, daily traded value USD 6m to 8m, technical momentum F, relative strength at the 20th percentile and the 200-day average falling for twelve months, which together say that the marginal holder since September has been a seller and that institutional interest is on hold until the accounts; Interogo's 40.2% and SEB's 6% are not sellers. The investor narrative is the broker narrative with less nuance ("aggressive accounting, too much debt, the deal engine will stop") and it has priced the stock at the IPO level and the 9th percentile of its multiple range.

Which dynamics matter most, and whether their being unknown favours the case. The two that favour the case and are unknown: the secured share of 2027 growth and the covenant definition. Both say the dominant fear, a forced halt to acquisitions within 18 months, is misplaced; the realistic bad case is a chosen slowdown that costs 3 to 5 points of 2028 EBITA growth, and the market has priced something worse. The two that threaten the case and are unknown: the Dutch insurer concentration (five payers, one-year contracts, West at 51% of sales; the 3% largest-contract figure understates it) and founder retention after the 2022 to 2023 cohort's earn-outs expire in 2026 to 2027 (undisclosed; peers' experience is that two or three hires replace one founder and the subsidiary's margin dilutes); neither is dated, neither is monitorable, and either would arrive as a surprise. The one that is unknown and neutral in direction but will produce a false signal: the absence of a fourth-quarter working-capital release, which will make the February 2027 cash print look worse against AddLife's than it is, and which the memo should pre-empt. Over 18 months to three years, the dynamics no one articulates are the ones that decide whether the compounder label is re-earned: founder retention and inventory discipline at the enlarged scale, and the structural margin ceiling of a brand-neutral distributor (10 to 12%), which the narrative treats as an Asker failing rather than a fact it shares with AddLife's cash margin of 11.7%.

Is the narrative wholly the earnings-quality narrative, so that clean accounts would be a positive surprise? Yes, with one qualification. The dominant voice has already moved from "sell" to "balanced", so the surprise from clean accounts is smaller than it would have been in September; but the price has not moved with the voice (SEK 70.8 against targets of SEK 66.5, 82 and 106), the ratings mix is two Holds and a Buy, and the forums have the CFO's sale as their anchor. A clean FY2026 report with a leverage re-definition and a disclosed capitalisation policy would surprise the price if not the broker; a mild clean-up with the same disclosures would be a one-day negative and a one-quarter positive, because the narrative already assumes a 20 to 25% overstatement of earnings and would receive a 5 to 7% one. The asymmetry favours the case: the narrative has priced the bear's reconstruction, and the base case is better than the reconstruction.
