Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Asker Healthcare Group AB (ASKER-SE, Nasdaq Stockholm), a decentralised European distributor of medical supplies, devices and equipment (not pharmaceuticals) to hospitals, primary care, elderly care and homecare, with more than 50 companies in 19 countries in three business areas, North (Sweden, Norway, Finland, the Baltics), West (Benelux, United Kingdom, Ireland, Denmark) and Central (Germany, Austria, Switzerland, France, Poland, Czech Republic, Slovakia), and two reported product segments, Medical Supplies, Devices and Equipment, and Services, growing through organic sales and 10 to 20 bolt-on acquisitions a year

Request: OWN-1

Question: For Asker Healthcare Group AB (ASKER-SE), what do the income-tax notes of its 2022 to 2025 annual reports (the 2025 annual report was published on 10 February 2026) show for the current tax charge, the deferred tax charge or credit, income tax paid in cash and profit before tax, year by year; how is the amortisation of acquired intangible assets (customer relationships, brands and distribution rights recognised in purchase-price allocations) treated for corporate income tax in Sweden, Norway, Finland, Denmark, the Netherlands, Belgium, Germany, Poland and the United Kingdom when, as at Asker, acquisitions are made as share purchases rather than asset purchases; how large is the deferred tax liability recognised on acquired intangibles in Asker's balance sheet and how fast does it unwind through the income statement; and what do AddLife AB, Lifco AB, Lagercrantz Group AB, Indutrade AB and Addtech AB disclose for income tax paid in cash relative to profit before tax and relative to profit before amortisation of acquired intangibles in 2023 to 2025, so that the cash tax rate a serial acquirer of this kind actually pays on its adjusted pre-tax profit can be established?

Request: OWN-2

Question: For Asker Healthcare Group AB (ASKER-SE), whose shares fell about 20% relative to AddLife AB between DNB Carnegie's downgrade to Sell on 10 September 2026 and its upgrade to Hold on 1 October 2026 and whose 24-month-forward price-to-earnings multiple stood at about 19.5x on 16 October 2026 against a listed-period median of about 24x (FactSet), what is the quarter-by-quarter record, over 2022 to 2026, of the forward EV/EBITA or price-to-earnings multiple, the reported net debt to EBITDA ratio, operating cash conversion and the number of acquisitions completed at AddLife AB (leverage peak at the end of 2023), Storskogen Group AB (leverage peak in late 2022), Vimian Group AB (leverage peak in mid-2022 and directed share issue) and Lifco AB (after the short report of 14 January 2026), and at any other European healthcare or industrial distributor that faced a public earnings-quality or leverage critique; in each case, how many quarters after the leverage or critique peak did the multiple begin to recover, which disclosed event (an annual report, a cash-flow print, a leverage threshold, a resumed acquisition, a capital markets day, a change of chair or chief financial officer) coincided with the first re-rating, how far the multiple recovered toward its prior level within 12 and 24 months, and in which cases it did not recover and why?

Request: OWN-3

Question: For Asker Healthcare Group AB (ASKER-SE), what have Swedish and Nordic financial media (Dagens Industri, Affärsvärlden, Placera, EFN, Börsvärlden and investing podcasts such as Börspodden and Fill or Kill), Redeye, posts on the Avanza and Nordnet shareholder forums, Twitter or X accounts that follow Nordic serial acquirers, and published fund-manager letters, monthly reports or holdings commentary said about the company since DNB Carnegie's downgrade of 10 September 2026 and its upgrade to Hold of 1 October 2026; which of the following each discusses and with what view: the capitalisation of development costs and the ten-year software useful life, the inventory obsolescence reserve, items affecting comparability, the exclusion of contingent consideration from reported net debt, the 3.50x bank leverage covenant disclosed in the prospectus of 17 March 2025, the Gothenburg distribution centre's timetable and double-running costs, the Dutch insurer-contracted direct-to-patient business, founder retention after earn-outs expire, the chief financial officer's sale of 225,000 shares on 29 May 2026, the new Chair's call options issued by Interogo Long-Term Equity, and the acquisition pace; which institutional holders have increased or reduced positions according to ownership filings, Holdings data and fund reports since September 2026; and whether short interest or securities-lending data for the share has risen since September 2026?
